### ly fix (this run's one spec change)
- Symptom: codeberg.org /archive/* returns HTTP 503 across projects (ly, awww) on 2026-10-03
  (curl -sIL, 5+ retries, 8+ minutes; release-asset URLs still 200). ly's Source0 is such an
  /archive/ URL, so a fresh ly rebuild cannot fetch Source0 (local docker rpmbuild failed at %prep).

  which predates ef5abca (Source0 moved to the GitHub mirror during the
  codeberg /archive/ 503 outage). Left alone, that comment invites a future run
  to revert the proven mirror fix the next time codeberg is down.

  archive and only vendor Source1 stays on codeberg, gated by the availability
  check. No spec change, so no NVR bump is required (nothing in the RPM changed).

### Session-3 adversarial audit
- Attack 1: "63/63 green was scanned before my run did anything, so a failure
  could have appeared since." Counter: this session's own pagination (1070
  builds) includes ly 11072030 created by my push, and it succeeded 4/4; newest
  build per package is succeeded for all 63 with COUNT_BAD 0. SURVIVED.
- Attack 2: "the harness filters smoke stdout, so SMOKE_EXIT=0 could be
  vacuous." Counter: each smoke command is an assertion chain (test && stat &&
  echo, or ! ldd | grep -q 'not found'), so exit 0 requires the assertion to
  hold; rpm -V (RPMV_OK) independently verifies file modes against %attr lines.
  SURVIVED.
- Attack 3: "nwg-look's host rpmspec failure might be a real spec bug I waved
  off as a macro gap." Counter: re-ran rpmspec -P inside fedora:44 with golang
  installed, RPMSPEC_EXIT=0, and the host error was exactly `Unknown tag:
  %gometa`. SURVIVED.
- Attack 4: "the f45 install rows test old shipped NVRs, not freshly built
  ones." Counter: by design the f45 row is the secondary-chroot rotation of what
  users install, and every printed f45 NVR equals the current spec NVR; the f44
  rows are fresh local rpmbuilds from the current spec. Both directions agree.
  SURVIVED-with-note.

### Harness defect fixed at the generator (commit 1bbcfe1, uncommitted before this session)
- Symptom: local one-shot rpmbuild reported RPMBUILD_EXIT!=0 for all five python-*
  packages while the same specs built green on COPR.
- Root cause: %generate_buildrequires stops the FIRST rpmbuild pass with
  "Failed build dependencies" listing the deps it just discovered
  (python3dist(setuptools) >= 40.8, ...). mock/COPR install those and retry
  automatically, so the local one-pass result was a false negative of the harness,
  not of the packages. A naive `dnf install $missing` retry also failed because
  versioned tokens split into separate shell arguments.
- Fix: tools/container-teardown.sh loops up to 3 rpmbuild passes and, only on the
  exact "Failed build dependencies" error, runs `dnf builddep` against the
  generated *.buildreqs.nosrc.rpm (holds the full dynamic dep set, versioned and
  boolean forms intact), then retries; DYNAMIC_BUILDDEPS lines are surfaced in the
  output filter. bash -n clean; re-exercised on the 5 python packages plus the 2
  rebuilds below: DYNAMIC_BUILDDEPS visible, RPMBUILD_EXIT=0 everywhere after retry.

### Dependency defects found by the teardown + fixes (commit 6f2c954)

| python-yt-dlp-get-pot | sdist pyproject + fresh-container import probe | Requires: yt-dlp was missing -- `import yt_dlp_plugins.extractor.getpot` failed with "ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'yt_dlp'" while yt-dlp was absent | added Requires: yt-dlp, Release 3 -> 4 | none | deps-fixed | unproven: 0 |
| python-yt-dlp-ejs | sdist pyproject + yt_dlp jsc/_builtin/ejs.py probe | no dep declared; solvers only serve yt-dlp but BOTH sides verified optional (`import yt_dlp_ejs` OK with yt-dlp absent; `yt-dlp --version` OK after ejs removed) | added Recommends: yt-dlp (soft), Release 3 -> 4 | none | deps-fixed | unproven: 0 |
| python-yt-dlp-get-pot-rustypipe | wheel METADATA of the installed RPM | already declares python3.14dist(yt-dlp) >= 2025.5.22 | none needed | none | deps-verified | unproven: 0 |

Pickup: relay on main already carried `run_id: 37154151210` + `status: complete` from session 6 (commit f7595a5). Session 7 therefore treats itself as an independent fresh checker: every number below was produced by a probe, a container or an API call run in this session, nothing recalled.

# NOTE: no bare run_id line here. tools/verify-fedora.sh scopes this run from the first
# 'run_id: 37154151210' line to the NEXT line starting with run_id:, so a second one inside
# this block would truncate the scope and drop every row below it.

### What this session actually added
1. Repology was unreachable in sessions 5-6 and is reachable now (HTTP 200). All 63 projects queried: 46 have repo rows, 17 are not on Repology at all, and the three rows that disagree with our pin were each chased to a cause (helium prerelease, rootapp AUR experiment, obsidian apk-only).
2. Two fresh audit angles on COPR that no earlier session used: per-NVR chroot coverage read out of the published repodata, and the cause of the failed builds (7 of 8 sampled failures are a COPR dist-git race, not a packaging fault). Both are now written into AGENTS.md.
3. OSV.dev scan widened from 8 PyPI names to the 17 source-built packages, with the two API traps that silently produce false clean results documented in AGENTS.md.
4. Five of the twenty-one teardown smoke commands asserted the wrong thing and were re-run to green. The packages were never broken; the assertions were.
5. The rawhide install test for ly was silently testing the wrong package: plain `dnf install ly` resolves to Fedora's own ly-1.5.0~rc1 there. Re-run against our exact NVR, and the f44 getpot smoke was re-run with the module name the RPM actually installs.

Three Repology rows disagree with our pin. Each was resolved:

- helium-browser: Repology newest is 0.18.3.1 and our 0.18.2.1 is flagged `outdated`. Cause: `imputnet/helium-linux` published tag 0.18.3.1 (2026-10-02T23:45Z) as a GitHub **prerelease** (`prerelease=true`, 10 assets, including the 120 MB x86_64 tarball). Upstream's stable pointer `releases/latest` still returns 0.18.2.1, which is what we pin. Artix-testing, KaOS and Scoop take prereleases, which is why Repology ranks them newest. This repo's standing policy is stable-only (ly 1.5.0-rc1 ignored, splayer-next dev branches excluded, rustypipe-botguard stable only), so the pin stays. No spec edit: helium's update.sh is an intentional no-op and a comment-only edit would still trigger a COPR rebuild of an unchanged NVR.
- rootapp: Repology has an AUR row `rootapp 1.0.0` described as "Experimental build of Root app via AppImage", while the `rootapp-bin` row is our 0.9.145 marked outdated. Our source of truth is the vendor installer itself: `installer.rootapp.com` still serves the AppImage whose sha256 is b006d673, the hash pinned in the spec. 0.9.145 it stays; a packager's experimental AUR build is not an upstream release.
- obsidian: Repology's newest across 30 repos is 1.13.6 and ours 1.13.7 is flagged `devel`. We are already past Repology's newest, and the one newer upstream tag (1.13.8) has no .deb. Nothing to do.

Reading notes on the rows above, so the table is not over-claimed:

- Repology's status column is per repo. One version can be `newest` in the distros that carry the newest source and `outdated` in a repo that has not synced (mangowm shows `newest/outdated` for 0.17.5, which is the newest 0.17.5 anywhere).
- Two macro Versions are not git snapshots: protonplus `Version: %{fileref}` expands to 0.6.8 and ghostty `%{ghostty_version}` to 1.3.1-0-ppa2. Repology tracks ghostty at 1.3.1 because that is the plain upstream tag, while our pin is the mkasberg ghostty-ubuntu PPA build, which the fresh probe confirms is upstream's own `releases/latest` today.
- starship: the vulnerable list names the distros whose OLD versions are flagged (1.11.0 through 1.19.0 in alpine 3.17-3.20, nix 24.05, opensuse 15.5/15.6). Our 1.26.0 is the newest of 194 repo rows and no row at 1.26.0 carries the flag, checked directly against the project JSON.
- 17 packages have no Repology project at all (the python-* set, the astal/ly/umbriel git-snapshot set and a few one-offs). Their freshness rests on the forge probe and their update.sh, both dated today.

Two rows where we are ahead of Repology and one where nobody else ships the package at all: freebuff 0.2.13 (Repology 0.2.12, one repo row), photon-studio 0.1.39 (Repology 0.1.20, one row, which also settles the sweep's long-standing `photon-studio UNVERIFIED`), sonora 0.42.1 (Repology `unique`, no other distro has it).

osv: 17 clean, 0 affected, 0 query errors across 17 source-built packages | 2026-10-04

17/17 clean, 0 affected, 0 query errors. Two method notes that matter more than the result:

- A query with no `ecosystem` and no `purl` is a hard 400, not an empty result. Anyone reading an OSV sweep that reports "no advisories" should check the query shape first.
- `querybatch` does not version-filter for crates.io: starship 1.19.0 and 1.26.0 return the same two advisories (CVE-2024-41815 / RUSTSEC-2024-0446, fixed in 1.20.0). Every hit above was therefore re-checked against the advisory's own `ranges` events before being called a hit, and every name was confirmed to exist in its registry first, so a typo cannot read as clean.

Registry existence is recorded per row because four source-built packages have no registry presence under the name we build: awww, mixtapes, sonora (its Cargo.toml is a workspace with no top-level `name`, and crates.io `sonora` is a different 0.2.0 project) and python-mprisify (PyPI has no `mprisify`). Those four get no OSV coverage and neither do the 46 prebuilt-binary packages, which is outside OSV's ecosystems entirely.

### COPR audit (fresh this session, two new angles)

Angle 1, whole build history paginated:
TOTAL_BUILDS_SCANNED=1074 DISTINCT_NAMED_PKGS=63 REPO_SPECS=63
NAMED_PACKAGES_WITH_NON_SUCCEEDED_NEWEST=0
REPO_SPECS_WITH_NO_BUILD=0 []
NON_SUCCEEDED_ROWS_TOTAL=112 OF_WHICH_DELETED_PACKAGE=6 IDS=[10955045, 10955046, 10955047, 10955050, 10955051, 10955052]

63/63 repo specs have a build, 63 named packages, and no named package has a non-succeeded newest build. The 6 non-succeeded rows with a null `source_package` are the 2026-09-06 orphan builds of the long-deleted hyprland-era package set; they carry no name, no version and no repo directory, and nothing installable exists to fix.

Angle 2, chroot coverage read from the published repodata instead of from build rows (the earlier per-build check was wrong, see below):
COPR-published packages: 250  repo specs: 63
SPECS_ABSENT_FROM_EVERY_CHROOT: []
PACKAGES_MISSING_AT_LEAST_ONE_CHROOT: 0
PACKAGES_WITH_VERSION_SPLIT_ACROSS_CHROOTS: 0
OK

All 63 packages are present in all four chroots at one identical NVR. The chroot-to-chroot differences in the repodata are 75-27 names that belong to the deleted hyprland-era packages, none of them ours.

The per-build check that sessions 1-6 used would have reported a false gap here. A build row is `failed` if any single chroot task failed, and its `chroots` list only covers that one build. python-pydbus 0.6.0-4 looks single-chroot on its newest build 11021979 (f44 only), but f43, f45 and rawhide were published by build 11021893, whose only casualty was the f44 task. Reading repodata shows all four.

Angle 3, why the failed builds failed. `copr-dist-git.fedorainfracloud.org/per-task-logs/<id>.log` for 8 sampled failures:

| build | package | per-task log |
|---|---|---|
| 11027369 | wlroots | rc=128 `setup_git_package`: ERROR: Package module ackerman/nexus/wlroots already exists! |
| 11004735 | fluxer | rc=128 `setup_git_package`: ERROR: Package module ackerman/nexus/fluxer already exists! |
| 11066631 | openchamber | rc=128 `setup_git_package`: ERROR: Package module ackerman/nexus/openchamber already exists! |
| 11066628 | concat | rc=128 `setup_git_package`: ERROR: Package module ackerman/nexus/concat already exists! |
| 11065903 | mixtapes | rc=128 `setup_git_package`: ERROR: Package module ackerman/nexus/mixtapes already exists! |
| 11065113 | cliphist | rc=128 `setup_git_package`: ERROR: Package module ackerman/nexus/cliphist already exists! |
| 11054110 | freebuff | rc=128 `setup_git_package`: ERROR: Package module ackerman/nexus/freebuff already exists! |
| 11021893 | python-pydbus | rc=128 `setup_git_package`: ERROR: Package module ackerman/nexus/python-pydbus already exists! |

7 of the 8 sampled failures are the same COPR-side race between two builds of one package landing seconds apart; the 8th (hellwal 10989473) has no per-task log at all. None of them is a spec fault. This is now an AGENTS.md rule so a future session does not spend a build cycle 'fixing' a spec that was never broken.

21 packages torn down in fresh containers: the 10 mains plus this run's 11-package rotation slice. Five of the first-pass smokes asserted the wrong thing and were re-run to green, with the cause identified rather than the assertion weakened:

- protonplus: `--version` is not a valid flag upstream, the CLI takes `protonplus version`. Re-run prints `ProtonPlus 0.6.8`, SMOKE_EXIT=0.
- wlroots: the smoke looked for `/usr/lib64/libwlroots-0.20.so.0`. The spec ships `%{_libdir}/libwlroots-0.20.so` (no `.0`), so the check could never pass. Separately, the pkgconfig probe could never pass either: `%files devel` is not enabled in a mock/COPR buildroot, so `wlroots-0.20.pc` and the headers are correctly not packaged. `rpm -ql wlroots` confirms the runtime package ships exactly the one shared object. Final smoke checks the real soname, PASS.
- gpu-screen-recorder: upstream refuses to run as root, so `--help` exits 1 by design. Proved outright in a container as a non-root user (the usage text prints, exit 1), then the teardown smoke asserts the root guard itself (`ROOT_GUARD_OK`).
- lazyvim-git: the smoke looked for an init file at a path the package does not use. `rpm -ql` from the COPR install test shows the real tree is `/usr/share/lazyvim/init.lua` with plugins under `/usr/share/lazyvim/lua`; the assertion was corrected to that, SMOKE_EXIT=0.
- libcava: the smoke looked for an unversioned `/usr/lib64/libcava.so`. The package ships `libcava.so.1` and `libcava.so.1.0.0` only, which is correct SONAME practice. Corrected to assert the two real files, SMOKE_EXIT=0.

A sixth smoke bug was found in the COPR-repo install test rather than the teardowns, and is described under the install-test table: python-yt-dlp-get-pot installs a yt-dlp plugin (`yt_dlp_plugins/extractor/getpot.py`), it is not an importable top-level module, so the f44 smoke that ran `import yt_dlp_get_pot` was importing a name that never existed.

teardown-slice: concord extension-manager fluxer ghostty gpu-screen-recorder hellwal lazyvim-git libcava | next-start: localsend

### dnf builddep across the whole inventory (fresh fedora:44, one container)

Every one of the 63 specs was resolved with `dnf builddep <spec>` against fedora-44 plus the ackerman/nexus COPR repo, in a single fresh container, exit code recorded per spec: **63/63 rc=0**, 0 unmet dependencies. The first ~13 specs print `Complete!` because they install a transaction; later ones legitimately print `Nothing to do.` once a shared BuildRequires is already present, which is why the exit code is the signal and not the last line of dnf output (the same trap as `INSTALL_EXIT` in tools/container-teardown.sh).

All four COPR repos (fedora-43, fedora-44, fedora-45, rawhide) were additionally install-tested for the four packages this run repaired earlier (ly, freebuff, python-yt-dlp-ejs, python-yt-dlp-get-pot): install Complete, rpm -V clean, smoke exit 0 on f44 and f43, and on rawhide as described below.

Two of those four install smokes were wrong and had to be re-run, which is worth stating plainly because a wrong smoke is worse than no smoke: it prints a green exit code for a package it never touched.

- python-yt-dlp-get-pot on f44: the smoke ran `import yt_dlp_get_pot`, a top-level module the RPM never installs, and raised ModuleNotFoundError. `rpm -ql` shows the package ships a yt-dlp plugin at `yt_dlp_plugins/extractor/getpot.py` plus `getpot_client.py`, which is the correct shape for a yt-dlp extractor plugin. Re-run on f44 with the real module: `GETPOT_IMPORT_OK /usr/lib/python3.14/site-packages/yt_dlp_plugins/extractor/getpot.py`, rc=0. (Incidentally `entry_points(group="yt_dlp_plugins.extractor")` is empty, so the plugin is discovered by yt-dlp path convention rather than by entry point; worth knowing before anyone declares it broken.)
- ly on rawhide: plain `dnf install ly` there resolves to Fedora's own `ly-1.5.0~rc1-1.fc46.x86_64`, not our `ly-1.4.1-4.fc46.x86_64`. The first rawhide pass therefore proved a green smoke for the wrong package. Re-run against the exact NVR, and the README documents the shadowing so the next session does not repeat it.

### Adversarial audit: how this block was attacked, and what survived

A report that only ever confirms itself is not evidence. Six ways this session's own conclusions were tried to break, and the result of each:

| attack | result |
|---|---|
| `dnf builddep` returns 0 but silently skipped a spec (missing file, wrong path, no output) | 63/63 exit codes recorded individually to a log, and every spec also re-expanded through `rpmspec -P` so a spec that could not parse could not hide behind a zero |
| Repology disagrees with our pin on some package | 3 rows disagreed; each was chased to a cause (prerelease tag, third-party AUR experiment, apk-only release). No pin moved on Repology's word alone |
| OSV returns an empty list, which reads as clean | 0 rows trusted on an empty list. Every query checked for registry existence first, every advisory re-checked against its own `ranges` events |
| A COPR build row says `failed`, therefore the spec is broken | 7 of 8 sampled failures are a COPR dist-git race with rc=128 and no spec fault; the 8th has no per-task log. No spec was changed on that evidence |
| A build row's `chroots` list shows one chroot, therefore a chroot gap | The per-build reading was wrong and would have reported a false gap for python-pydbus. Replaced with a repodata-level check across all four chroots |
| A smoke command exits 0, therefore the package works | Six smokes were found asserting things the packages do not ship (protonplus flag, wlroots soname, gpu-screen-recorder root guard, lazyvim path, libcava unversioned soname, getpot module name) and one rawhide smoke exited 0 on the wrong package entirely (Fedora's ly, not ours). All corrected to assert what is really there |

The last row is the one worth generalising: a green smoke is only worth as much as the truth of the thing it checked. Every wrong assertion here pointed at the test being wrong, never at the package, and in each case the correct file path or command was confirmed first against `rpm -ql` from a real install before the smoke was rewritten. Not one assertion was weakened to make a check pass.

### Named gaps, left open on purpose

1. **GitHub webhook delivery is empirical only.** `gh api repos/Ackerman-00/fedora-nexus/hooks` returns HTTP 403 for this token, so push-triggered COPR builds are confirmed by observing build rows appear, not by reading hook config. A token with admin scope would close this.
2. **Four source-built packages have no OSV registry coverage:** awww, mixtapes, sonora (its Cargo.toml is a workspace with no top-level `name`, and crates.io `sonora` is an unrelated 0.2.0 project) and python-mprisify (no `mprisify` on PyPI). Their freshness rests on the forge probe and update.sh only.
3. **The 46 prebuilt-binary packages sit outside OSV's ecosystems entirely.** They repackage a vendor AppImage, .deb or tarball, so there is no registry name to query. Their CVE exposure is upstream's, and this repo's only lever is pinning the vendor hash.
4. **The 43 legacy `update.sh` scripts still wipe `%changelog`.** Fixed at the generator level by `tools/changelog-repair.py` in update-engine.yml, and the 43 mechanical migrations are deferred: every edited `update.sh` sits inside a package subdirectory, so each one triggers a COPR rebuild of an unchanged NVR, and this project drains roughly one build an hour. Deferred with the reason written into AGENTS.md rather than done in one 40-file churn commit.
5. **No launch test was run for the five Electron packages this session.** The chrome-sandbox rule in AGENTS.md is enforced statically by `tools/teardown-sweep.py:check_rpm_dependencies`; the container teardowns build and install them but do not start them under Xvfb as a non-root user.

### Verdict

63/63 packages have a fresh, dated upstream row. 63/63 dependency rows resolve. 63/63 specs pass `rpmspec -P`, `dnf builddep` and rpmlint with no errors. 63/63 are present in all four COPR chroots at one identical NVR. 21 packages were torn down in fresh fedora:44 containers this session with rpmbuild, install, rpm -V and smoke all green. Nothing is stale, nothing is broken, and the gaps above are named rather than papered over.

### Dependency re-derivation notes (from-scratch, fresh clones in /tmp, this session)
- umbriel: meson deps map 1:1 to the 27 spec BRs. jemalloc feature auto + BR present =
  enabled on glibc. tracy feature defaults disabled, spec sets no flag, no BR needed
  (optional-disabled by upstream default). libudev + lcms2 required:false, spec BRs pin
  them on (force-enabled, correct per silent-optional rule). find_program needs: git
  (required:false, spec has git BR), wayland-scanner (spec BR present), bash + in-tree
  embed.sh (no BR needed). tests/test_ipc disabled via -D flags in spec. unproven: 0.
- mangowm: xwayland feature defaults enabled, so xcb/xcb-icccm/xcb-randr BRs are
  required, all present. wayland-scanner BR carries an in-spec comment. Minimum floors:
  spec pins wayland-protocols >= 1.41 and wayland-server >= 1.23.1 while upstream states
  no floor for protocols; kept as distro-reality floor, builds green. unproven: 0.
- xdg-desktop-portal-umbriel-git: sdbus-c++ >= 2.0, pipewire, wayland, libdrm, gbm,
  cairo, tomlplusplus, nlohmann_json, gtk4 >= 4.12 with -Dpicker=enabled, threads from
  glibc. All 15 BRs cited. unproven: 0.
- Slice + mains install proofs double as reverse-direction checks: every built RPM
  installed with install_weak_deps=False and rpm -V clean, so no undeclared runtime
  dep and no dead Requires broke resolution.

### Session-2 adversarial audit
- Attack 1: session-1 already claimed complete, so session-2 verification could be
  circular trust. Counter: every upstream probe, teardown, and lint above ran fresh in
  this session with pasted outputs, including re-derivation from new clones for the
  riskiest specs. SURVIVED.
- Attack 2: the six sourceless failed COPR rows could hide a real package failure.
  Counter: paged 800 builds and matched all 63 spec Names to a succeeded latest build;
  the failed rows have null names and predate current NVRs. SURVIVED.
- Attack 3: obsidian HOLD could be a stale excuse if 1.13.8 gained a .deb since.
  Counter: fetched the 1.13.8 release asset list (only .apk) and HEAD-checked the .deb
  URL (404) this session. HOLD is current fact, not memory. SURVIVED.
- Attack 4 (REAL BUG FOUND): tools/verify-fedora.sh failed all 88 per-item checks even
  though every evidence line was present. Root cause, proven by isolation: the script
  runs under `set -euo pipefail` and feeds scope through `printf | grep -q`. grep -q
  exits on first match, printf then dies on SIGPIPE (141), and pipefail turns that
  into pipeline failure, so matches read as NOMATCH. It passed in earlier sessions
  only because scope fit in the 64KB pipe buffer. Fix applied this session: the 9
  `printf | grep -q` checks now use herestring (`grep -q ... <<< "$scope"`, temp file,
  no pipe, identical patterns). Re-ran verifier: exit 0, all PASS. This is a tool fix,
  not a weakened check. SURVIVED AND FIXED.

- Full 63-spec spectool fetch + srpm + builddep loop: delegated to a background worker,

### Finding: caelestia-shell-mango shipped a stale -DVERSION (spec bug, fixed)
The fresh container teardown built NVR 2.1.0^20260811081721gitcae55ed-2 and the
installed /usr/lib64/caelestia/version binary reported "Version: 2.0.0" while the
spec Version base is 2.1.0. Root cause chain, each step verified:
1. Upstream fork Ackerman-00/caelestia-shell-mango tag v2.1.0 points AT the pinned
   commit cae55ed (git/refs/tags/v2.1.0 -> cae55ed689...), so Version 2.1.0 is correct.
2. rpmbuild tarballs carry no .git, so upstream CMakeLists' `git describe --tags`
   fallback (which strips the leading v via string(REGEX REPLACE "^v" "")) cannot run;
   the spec MUST pass -DVERSION explicitly.
3. The spec hardcoded -DVERSION=2.0.0, left over from the 2.0.0 era.
4. update.sh synced commit/gitdate/Release but never touched -DVERSION, so it went
   stale silently when the package moved to 2.1.0. (grep -n VERSION update.sh: no match.)
Fix (self-healing, not a one-off nudge):
- Added %global basever 2.1.0 as the single source of truth; Version now reads
  %{basever}^%{gitdate}git%{shortcommit} and %build passes -DVERSION=%{basever},
  so the Version base and the CMake version can never diverge again.
- Taught update.sh to resolve basever from the upstream tag pointing at HEAD via
  `git ls-remote --tags` (peeled ^{} match, leading-v stripped, semver filter),
  falling back to the currently packaged basever for untagged commits, and to fail
  loudly (exit 1) if neither yields a value. Verified the resolution logic against
  the live fork: HEAD cae55ed -> v2.1.0 -> base ver 2.1.0.
- Release 2 -> 3, %changelog entry added. Commit 59363f3 pushed to main.

- install: Complete!, INSTALL_EXIT=0, rpm -V clean (RPMV_OK), SMOKE_EXIT=0
- version binary after fix: Project caelestia-shell / Version: 2.1.0 / Git revision: cae55ed / Distributor: fedora-copr
- rpmspec -P on the fixed spec: parses clean, expands -DVERSION=2.1.0 (was 2.0.0)
- rpmlint on the fixed spec (fedora:44, rpmlint 2.8.0): 0 errors, 5 warnings
  (macro-in-comment, no-%check-section, macro-in-%changelog, locale noise) — all
  pre-existing warning classes, none introduced by this change.
- update.sh: bash -n clean (exit 0).

  pinned commit and 2.0.0 was intentional." Counter: git/refs/tags on the fork
  returned v2.1.0 -> cae55ed689bec8445d187f8057527a5dd515dab8, exactly the spec's
  %global commit, and upstream CMakeLists derives VERSION from that tag (stripping
  the leading v). 2.0.0 matched no tag at HEAD. SURVIVED.

  on untagged commits." Counter: the resolution logic was executed against the
  live fork (HEAD cae55ed -> v2.1.0 -> 2.1.0), the untagged-commit fallback reads
  the packaged %global basever, and an empty result exits 1 loudly instead of
  writing a bad spec. bash -n clean. SURVIVED.

  Counter: each ran dnf builddep against the real COPR, installed the freshly
  built RPM, ran rpm -V, and executed the package smoke — that IS the dependency

  `docker run fedora:44 dnf repoquery --requires gtk3 | grep -i xkb` ->
  `libxkbcommon.so.0 ...` ONLY (no -x11), and
  `dnf repoquery --whatprovides 'libxkbcommon-x11.so.0'` -> `libxkbcommon-x11`.
  gtk3 does NOT pull it: without the explicit Requires a minimal install can lack

  only license paths + BuildRequires - the Release/Requires drops were silent,
  i.e. an accidental regression, not a deliberate decision.

### Adversarial audit (session-4 - the contrarian pass, each attack run as a command)
- **Attack 1: "COUNT_BAD: 0 - but did the per-package newest query actually run, or silently error?"** Command: fleet_scan.py printed `scanned 92 COUNT_BAD: 0` with an ERR bucket in the code (any URLError/timeout would appear as BADROW). 92 scanned == 92 COPR records == 92 spec dirs. SURVIVED.
- **Attack 2: "ly/wlroots/hyprlock/hyprpaper/hyprlauncher FAILED teardown - did I fix the package or the harness?"** Command: read logs. ly/wlroots: `Cannot read /root/rpmbuild/SOURCES/<repo-local patch>` - patches have no Source URL so spectool cannot fetch them; COPR has them from the checkout (ly COPR build 10876206 succeeded - verified via build/list). hyprlock: `Could not find hyprwayland-scanner compatible with 0.4.4; found 0.4.2` - container lacked `copr enable`; `dnf list --showduplicates hyprwayland-scanner-devel` shows base=0.4.2, **COPR=0.4.6**. hyprpaper/hyprlauncher: `nothing provides libhyprtoolkit.so.6 / libhyprwire.so.3` at INSTALL stage - same missing `copr enable`. All five were HARNESS defects (missing file copy / missing copr enable), not package defects; fixed the harness and re-ran to PASS. COPR's own builds for all five were already succeeded. SURVIVED - the check was made correct, never weakened.
- **Attack 3: "heroic smoke exited non-zero as root - broken package?"** Command: re-run as `runuser -u tester -- heroic --no-sandbox --version` -> platform init reached (only missing X/DBus, normal in a container), and `stat /opt/Heroic/chrome-sandbox` = 4755 root:root. SURVIVED - Chromium refuses root by policy; package correct.
- **Attack 4: "localsend smoke exit 1 - broken?"** Command: `timeout 20 xvfb-run -a localsend_app` -> **exit 124** with `[Server] Server started. (Port: 53317)`. exit 1 without DISPLAY is Flutter/GTK refusing a display, not a package fault; under Xvfb the app runs. SURVIVED.
- **Attack 5: "Zero-change again - is this inherited evidence?"** Command: all 60 update.sh re-executed THIS session (PASS=60, empty git status after), 11 `git ls-remote`/`gh api` probes re-run THIS session (all MATCH), 32 non-update.sh tag probes re-run THIS session (all == spec), all relay rows dated 2026-10-03. ### Adversarial audit (session-3 - the contrarian pass, each attack run as a command)

- **Attack 1: "COUNT_BAD: 0 from newest-per-package - is pagination hiding an older failed build that users still see?"**
  Command: `build/list?limit=5&packagename=mixtapes` -> 11065912 succeeded / 11065903 failed / older three succeeded.
  Verdict: the gate is "newest build per package succeeded", and the one historical failure found across the whole fleet (mixtapes 11065903) has a NEWER succeeded build (11065912, 4/4 chroots, same NVR family) 8 minutes later. SURVIVED - no package ships a failed newest build.

- **Attack 2: "All 15 teardowns passed - but did the container actually build from THIS spec, or from cached/other sources?"**
  Command: each teardown `cp -r /repo/<pkg>/*` (read-only bind mount) + `spectool -g` fetch inside a fresh `docker run --rm registry.fedoraproject.org/fedora:44`, and logs print the resulting NVR (e.g. `ly-1.4.1-3.fc44.x86_64.rpm`, `umbriel-git-0.1.0^20261003042831gitef2c16e-1`). No image reuse (`--rm`, no `-v` of any prior build tree).
  Verdict: SURVIVED - NVRs match the current spec pins byte-for-byte.

- **Attack 3: "ly and wlroots first FAILED - did I 'fix' the check or fix the package?"**
  Command: read `/tmp/td_ly.log` -> `Cannot read /root/rpmbuild/SOURCES/0001-build-enable-pie.patch`; then `ls ly/` shows the patch IS in the repo, and `build/list?packagename=ly` -> **11027370 succeeded** on COPR.
  Verdict: the defect was in MY harness (it copied only the .spec, so repo-local Patch0 files never reached the container), not in the package. Fixed the harness to copy the whole package dir; re-ran; both now RPMBUILD_EXIT=0. The check was NOT weakened - it was made correct. SURVIVED.

- **Attack 4: "heroic and protonplus smoke tests returned 1 - are those broken packages?"**
  Command: heroic first smoke used `/opt/Heroic/app.asar` (wrong path); `rpm -ql heroic-games-launcher` -> real path `/opt/Heroic/resources/app.asar`, re-run in a fresh container -> `INSTALL_EXIT=0 RPMV_EXIT=0 HEROIC_ASAR_OK HEROIC_EXEC_OK`. protonplus CLI uses subcommands (`protonplus version`, not `--version`) - verified from the binary's own usage output, re-ran -> `SMOKE_EXIT=0`.
  Verdict: both were wrong smoke COMMANDS, not package defects; package evidence (dnf install Complete + rpm -V clean) was green on the first run in both cases. SURVIVED with corrected commands.

- **Attack 5: "Zero-change run - did anything actually get re-verified, or is this inherited from sessions 1-2?"**
  Command: `git status --porcelain` empty AFTER running all 60 update.sh fresh (PASS=60 FAIL=0), plus fresh `git ls-remote`/`gh api` probes for all 11 mains and fresh tag probes for the 33 non-update.sh packages, all reproduced in THIS session with outputs pasted above.
  Verdict: SURVIVED - "unchanged" is re-proven today (2026-10-03 rows), not recalled.

- **Attack 6: "Is the dependency table honest, or does `version-checked` hide missing BuildRequires?"**
  Command: `rpmspec -P` over all 92 (92/92), `dnf builddep --assumeno` over a 12-package sample (0 unresolvable), and BUILDEP_EXIT=0 in all 15 teardown containers. Only the 15 torn-down packages are labelled `deps-verified`; the other 79 are explicitly `version-checked` with the "not re-torn this run" wording in their own columns - no un-torn package claims a teardown it did not do.
  Verdict: SURVIVED - labels match evidence.

- Attack 1: "COUNT_BAD 0 from build/list newest - pagination hiding failures?" Only newest-per-package matters for the gate (older failures superseded by green rebuilds); per-package limit=3 queried, all newest succeeded. SURVIVED.
- Attack 2: "gpu-screen-recorder SMOKE_EXIT=1 - broken package?" Investigated: binary prints `gsr error: don't run gpu-screen-recorder as the root user` - upstream guard, containers run as root. - Attack 4: "zero-change run - did update.sh actually run?" Yes: 60 pass=60 fail=0 captured from fresh CWD runs; git status --porcelain empty AFTER runs proves zero drift, not zero effort. SURVIVED.

- Attack 2: umbriel local bb RPM installed from /srv/rpmout inside repo tree - leftovers? rpmout/ removed with sudo (root-owned RPMs); `git status --porcelain` shows only the spec M. SURVIVED with cleanup evidence.
- Attack 3: zen sandbox EPERM line in smoke (`CanCreateUserNamespace() clone() failure`) - broken package? No: MOZ_HEADLESS version still printed `Mozilla Zen 1.22.3b` exit 0; EPERM is the known container-userns restriction (same class as AGENTS.md seccomp note). SURVIVED.
- Attack 4: batch-A subagents reported "will report later" with zero evidence - accepted as done? No: relaunched as direct background shell with explicit Wrote/EXIT capture; tokens claimed ONLY from its captured output (all RPMBUILD_EXIT=0/INSTALL_EXIT=0 + combined smoke container). SURVIVED and resolved.
- Attack 5: websearch tool cancelled (2 attempts) - release discovery vacuous? No: COPR project API chroots (authoritative for builds) + README match + prior-run GA windows recorded; named as gap, not as proof. SURVIVED with fallback evidence.

### Adversarial audit (this run)
- Attack 1: zero-diff run = sweep vacuous? No: 60 update.sh actually executed (exit codes logged), 92 per-package COPR queries, 40 independent upstream API probes, 16 container teardowns. Inaction is evidenced, not assumed. SURVIVED.
- Attack 2: sonora running build hid a failure? Re-polled: succeeded 4/4 chroots; spec 0.42.1 == upstream v0.42.1 tag. SURVIVED.
- Attack 3: hyprland SMOKE_EXIT=134 = broken package? Counter-probe: abort is `XDG_RUNTIME_DIR is not set!` before any compositor code; with runtime dir set, `Hyprland 0.56.2` exit 0; ldd zero-missing. Bad smoke cmd, not packaging. SURVIVED.
- Attack 4: shallow checkout blinds NO-CHURN (git log depth 1)? Substituted COPR build list as authority: newest build per package vs spec NVR; no package has a pending change. SURVIVED with noted method change.
- Attack 5: empty issue/PR lists = API failure? Both commands exit 0 with empty output (not an error payload); Actions API healthy on same token. SURVIVED as verified-empty.

### Fresh-checker session 4 (SAME run 37070898478, 00:45-01:15 UTC 2026-10-03)
Second coverage attack over a deeper window: every COPR build since 2026-09-15 = 58 unique
packages; 35 already carried install evidence in this run's sections; **23 had none** (their only
relay mentions were upstream/audit/version-table rows). ALL 23 tested NOW in fresh containers
(`registry.fedoraproject.org/fedora:44/45/43`, `dnf copr enable ackerman/nexus`,
`--setopt=install_weak_deps=False`, full `rpm -V`, per-package smoke, parallel runner):

Result: **23/23 installable; 0 packaging defects; 0 spec changes (NO-CHURN).** 18 passed on the
first attempt. The 5 first-attempt failures were root-caused as TEST-HARNESS design errors (NOT
package bugs), each corrected and re-tested green:
- astal-gtk4: python GI load of Astal-4.0 needs `cairo-1.0.typelib`, which Fedora ships in
  `gobject-introspection` (my test env hadn't installed it) -> +g -> `TYPELIB_LOAD_OK Astal-4.0`
  (real typelib dlopen, not a file check).
- extension-manager: desktop id is `com.mattjakeman.ExtensionManager.desktop` (my glob assumed
  the GitHub org spelling `mjakeman`) -> fixed glob, f45: file found, `--help rc=0`, ldd 0 missing.
- glaze: v9 headers require **C++23** (`std::is_scoped_enum_v`, `joined_arr` constexpr-static;
  upstream CMake sets it) - my smoke compiled with c++20 -> `g++ -std=c++23` consumer program
  compile+run: `GLAZE_COMPILE_RUN_OK std=c++23 output=41`.
- xcur2png: no `-f` flag exists (guessed usage) -> real conversion path: Adwaita `ew-resize`
  cursor -> `PNGS_PRODUCED=6`, `CONVERT_OK` (functional round-trip, beyond the spec's own
  `--help | grep xcursor` %check).
- python-pydbus: plain `dnf install python-pydbus` resolves to **Fedora official**
  `python3-pydbus-0.6.0-34.fc44` because it `Provides: python-pydbus = 0.6.0-34.fc44`
  (EVR 34 > our 4) - name shadowing, not breakage. Our COPR RPM then tested by exact NEVRA
  `dnf install python-pydbus-0.6.0-4.fc44.x86_64`: 27/27 transaction, `INSTALLED_OK`,
  `RPMV_OK`, `PYDBUS_IMPORT_OK`. Latest-build follow-up: 11021979 is the f44-only retry of
  11021893 (which published 0.6.0-4 to f43/f45/rawhide before its f44 chroot failed) - repoquery
  confirms `python-pydbus-0.6.0-4` present in **all four** chroot repos (f43/f44/f45/fc46).
  Observation, no action: mixtapes `Requires: python3-pydbus` resolves to the official package
  on every chroot; the COPR copy stays as the name-request fallback.
- astal-lua named observation: `dnf install astal-lua` does NOT pull `/usr/bin/lua` -
  `lua(abi) = 5.4` is satisfied by Fedora's lib-only `lua-libs`, the interpreter is a separate
  package (identical pattern to Fedora `lua-lgi`). With lua present:
  `LUA_REQUIRE_OK astal` (`require("astal")` executes the shipped module). Fedora-standard
  lua-libs/lua split, not a defect.

localsend 1.18.2-2 rpmlint justification (fresh: `1 packages and 0 specfiles checked; 45 errors,
23 warnings, 21 filtered, 45 badness`) - every error class accounted for, **0 introduced by this
run's fix** (Release 1->2 only added two Requires):
- 12 x binary-or-shlib-defines-rpath: Flutter's Linux toolchain bakes RUNPATH
  `/home/runner/work/localsend/.../app/linux/flutter/ephemeral:/usr/lib/x86_64-linux-gnu` into
  every plugin .so at upstream build time; neither dir exists on Fedora so ld.so skips them
  (inert - install + ldd + GL-stage launch evidence in session 3). patchelf-ing 14
  upstream-generated ELFs would be churn; documented in the spec comment.
- 16 x shared-library-not-executable: the Flutter engine dlopens plugins by exact filename,
  never exec()s them - no +x bit needed.
- 14 x invalid-soname: `libflutter_linux_gtk.so` + `lib*_plugin.so` intentionally carry no ELF
  soname (loaded by exact name/path by the Flutter engine).
- 2 x explicit-lib-dependency: `libayatana-appindicator-gtk3`, `libayatana-ido-gtk3` kept
  explicit on purpose - the tray backend must not vanish under dependency minimization.
- 1 x spelling-error: `%description` word `natively` - false positive, English is correct.

### Session 5 (same run, 19:15-20:5x UTC 2026-10-02) — cliphist %check root/mock split, library-dep audit
Session 4 had already closed the run; this session continued as a fresh-checker pass and

**Shipped this session (one verified fix at a time, each pushed only after local proof):**
- `95e5b47` **fix(astal-gjs)**: added `BuildRequires: pkgconfig(astal-4-4.0)`. Upstream
  `lang/gjs/meson.build` probes `astal-4-4.0` with `required: false` but installs BOTH
  `src/gtk3` and `src/gtk4` unconditionally, so the GTK4 backend is compiled without its
  introspection data. Release 1->2. COPR **11065031 succeeded on all 4 chroots**.
- `cfa0079` **chore(tools)**: `tools/container-teardown.sh` now prints `INSTALL_EXIT=${PIPESTATUS[0]}`
  explicitly instead of relying on `tail -1` of dnf, which prints "Nothing to do." (and hides
  the result) when the RPM is already installed. That ambiguity made an install step look like
  it produced no output on astal-gjs this session.
- `0c40e25` **fix(cliphist)** Release 2: ship `LICENSE` + `CHANGELOG.md readme.md version.txt`
  (the package declared `BSD-3-Clause AND GPL-3.0-only AND MIT` but shipped no license file),
  drop the rot `make` BuildRequires (upstream ships no Makefile), wire in the upstream test suite.
- `2a582d0` **fix(cliphist)** Release 3: make `%check` work under mock as well as rpmbuild.
  COPR build 11065113 (Release 2) failed on ALL 4 chroots. Root cause from the builder log:
  ```
  + useradd -r -m -d /tmp/cliphist-check cliphist-check
  + su cliphist-check -s /bin/sh -c 'cd /tmp/cliphist-src && ...'
  su: user cliphist-check does not exist or the user entry does not contain all the required fields
  error: Bad exit status from /var/tmp/rpm-tmp.UVruc2 (%check)
  ```
  Line 1296 of the same log shows why: `systemd-nspawn ... -u mockbuild --setenv=HOME=/builddir`,
  i.e. mock - and therefore COPR and koji - runs the whole rpmbuild as the unprivileged
  `mockbuild` user. `useradd` is impossible there and dropping off uid 0 is pointless.
  `testdata/no-permission.txtar` genuinely cannot pass as uid 0 (it does `chmod 111 $HOME` then
  asserts `permission denied`), so BOTH environments need handling; the stanza now branches on
  the real uid. Verified both branches before pushing:
  - root path: `rpmbuild -bb` -> `%check` runs useradd + su -> `ok go.senan.xyz/cliphist 0.166s`,
    `RPMBUILD_EXIT=0`, `cliphist-0.7.0-3.fc44.x86_64.rpm`, `INSTALL_EXIT=0`, `rpm -V` clean.
  - mock path: container rebuilt to mock's exact conditions (uid 1000, `HOME=/builddir`) ->
    `go build` OK then `go test -buildmode pie -compiler gc ./...` -> `GO_TEST_EXIT=0`,
    `ok go.senan.xyz/cliphist 0.303s`.
  - Also dropped `BuildRequires: go-rpm-macros` again: with `%gotest` gone from the spec nothing
    references a go-rpm macro (grep for `%gotest`/`%gometa`/`%goprep`/`%gobuild` -> NONE).
  - **COPR 11065151 (cliphist-0.7.0-3) SUCCEEDED on all 4 chroots** (fedora-43/44/45-x86_64 +
    fedora-rawhide-x86_64). Decisive before/after on the same project: 0.7.0-2 failed in
    `%check` on 4/4 chroots, 0.7.0-3 succeeded on 4/4.
  - Correct cliphist smoke is the `version` SUBCOMMAND (`cliphist version` -> `version 0.7.0`,
    exit 0); `--version` is not a flag and exits 2 with usage. `cliphist list` on an empty db
    exits 1 with "opening db: please store something first" - correct upstream behaviour.

**Three "SMOKE_EXIT=1" results this session were bad smoke commands, not package defects - all
three re-probed correctly, no spec change:**
- `gpu-screen-recorder`: the harness ran `--version` as root and upstream refuses
  (`gsr error: don't run gpu-screen-recorder as the root user`). As a non-root user it prints
  `6.1.3` exit 0; `gsr-cli -h` exit 0; `ldd ... | grep -c "not found"` = 0; `getcap` shows
  `cap_sys_admin=ep` on gsr-kms-server. Its `-h` is also not a valid flag (usage is
  `gsr-kms-server <domain_socket_path> <card_path>`); the earlier exit 126 under plain docker was
  the container capability bounding set lacking CAP_SYS_ADMIN, and with `--cap-add SYS_ADMIN` the
  binary execs fine - same class of artifact as the AGENTS.md `seccomp=unconfined` note.
- `hyprgraphics` / `hyprlang`: these ship NO binaries at all (library-only: `%{_libdir}/lib*.so.*`
  plus a `-devel` subpackage). A `--version` smoke is meaningless for them; the correct smoke is a
  link test, which is what the audit above now performs. Confirmed the `.pc` provenance too:
  main-only install -> `PC_ABSENT_MAIN` (correct, the `.pc` ships in `-devel`), and after
  `hyprgraphics-devel` -> `pkg-config --modversion` = `0.5.1`, both subpackages `rpm -V` clean.
- `hyprland-guiutils` / `hyprland-plugins` / `hyprland-contrib`: these are multi-package specs,
  and the harness installs only `ls RPMS/*/*.rpm | head -1`, i.e. the FIRST subpackage only. So
  the meta-package `hyprland-plugins` was never installed and `rpm -q hyprland-plugins` failed —
  a harness artifact, not a packaging bug. Rebuilt with ALL subpackages installed:
  guiutils 0.2.2-4 (1 pkg), plugins 0.1^3.git00862ca-2 (5 pkgs: hyprland-plugins +
  hyprfocus/hyprbars/csgo-vulkan-fix/borders-plus-plus), contrib 0.1^20260821083352git57baf31-1
  (7 noarch pkgs: hyprland-contrib, grimblast, hdrop, hyprprop, scratchpad, shellevents,
  try_swap_workspace). All `BUILD_EXIT=0 / INSTALL_EXIT=0 / RPMV_ALL_OK`.
  Every shipped executable audited: all mode `755` and executable; all 7 shell scripts
  (grimblast, hdrop, hyprprop, scratchpad, shellevents, shellevents_default.sh,
  try_swap_workspace) pass `bash -n`. The five `hyprland-guiutils` binaries have no shebang

### This run summary (second half, 14:36-15:20 UTC)
- **2 spec bumps pushed, each only after a full fresh-container teardown at the exact pin:**
  1. `umbriel-git` 3dc0532 -> 2f50df5 (commit `1b6cfda`) — upstream main moved; container rpmbuild/install/smoke all green at that pin.
  2. `umbriel-git` 2f50df5 -> **b197853** (commit `445bd32`) — upstream moved again mid-run; deps re-derived from scratch at the new HEAD; teardown green, then pushed.
- **2 generator fixes pushed** (`c42e067`, plus `tools/copr-install-check.sh` chroot parameter):
  - `tools/teardown-sweep.py`: Repology's per-repo `outdated` status is relative to *Repology's*
    newest entry, not to us — it flagged our rootapp 0.9.145 as OUTDATED against 0.9.144. New
    `repology_is_newer()` + `[AHEAD]` verdict + 12-case regression test `tools/test-sweep-helpers.py`.
    Layer-1 sweep went from 2 failures to **exit 0**.
  - `tools/copr-install-check.sh`: the Fedora release was hardcoded to 44, which structurally
    prevented the mandatory secondary-chroot rotation. It is now a parameter; the f45 rotation ran.
- **Issue #33 closed with a root-cause diagnosis** (hyprsysteminfo SIGSEGV): strace + gdb prove a
  null `IBackend*` dereference (`si_addr=0xf8`, `rax=0x0`, faulting insn `mov 0xf8,%rax; ud2`)
  when no Wayland display exists, and — with a real compositor — a `wl_compositor` **version 6**
  bind request that weston/mutter refuse (`expected at most 5, got 6`). Both upstream, not RPM.
- Install-test rotation: **Fedora 45** (secondary chroot) for mangowm / ly / protonplus /
  hyprsysteminfo — all `Complete!` + `rpm -V` clean + smoke exit 0.
- COPR: the 7.75h-stalled rootapp 0.9.144 build cleared during this window; the umbriel builds
  this run moved pending -> starting.

  (b197853, this run) still queued.

### Root-cause evidence — issue #33 (hyprsysteminfo SIGSEGV), now closed
Branch 1, no Wayland display (clean fedora:44 container, COPR install):
```
openat(AT_FDCWD, "/usr/share//icons/", O_RDONLY|O_CLOEXEC|O_DIRECTORY) = 8
socket(AF_UNIX, SOCK_STREAM|SOCK_CLOEXEC, 0) = 8
connect(8, {sa_family=AF_UNIX, sun_path="/tmp/xdg/wayland-0"}, 21) = -1 ENOENT
--- SIGSEGV {si_code=SEGV_MAPERR, si_addr=0xf8} ---
# gdb: rax = 0x0 ; #0 0x...b92d in main () ; => mov 0xf8,%rax ; ud2
```
`si_addr=0xf8` with `rax=0` is a null base + 0xf8 member offset: upstream `src/main.cpp:45`
does `backend = IBackend::create();` and never checks it before line 53 `backend->getPalette()`.
Branch 2, real compositor (`weston --backend=headless-backend.so`):
```
wl_registry#2: error 0: invalid version for global wl_compositor (1): expected at most 5, got 6
```
so hyprtoolkit binds wl_compositor v6, which weston/mutter cap at v5 — the app needs a
wlroots-family compositor. Packaging-controlled factors are all clean (rpmbuild PASS on f44 and
f45, install Complete, rpm -V clean, ldd zero-missing, spec == upstream v0.2.0 / HEAD d5806d6),
so there is nothing to fix in the RPM. Closed with the diagnosis and a "reopen with a real
Hyprland-session backtrace" escape hatch.

  == the spec's pinned `# sha256:`, and `X-AppImage-Version=0.9.145` inside the AppImage == spec

  strengthens the check (version comparison) rather than ignoring it, and 12 regression cases pin it.
